#6929. St. Louis school board votes to close 9 schools

Let K-12 Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The move comes as the Missouri district faces declining enrollment. Its board of education may consider other closures in the future.

Before the 4-3 vote to close the nine schools, St. Louis board members discussed the reasons for the move, including a need to consolidate resources by shuttering underenrolled schools and to improve student outcomes. Some board members blamed private school choice and historic underinvestments in local public schools for the position the district finds itself in.

The vote took place during a marathon meeting where board members considered several other recommendations to the district’s Future Ready SLPS initiative. That plan addresses financial stability, academic improvements, operational efficiencies and boosting attendance.

The recommendations also cover school repurposing, grade level configurations, school designations, school start times, and expanded career and technical education opportunities. Many of the proposed changes would begin in the 2027-28 school year, with additional transitions planned for future years.

At Tuesday's meeting, board members decided to decline, postpone or gather additional information on some recommendations.

In a Nov. 11 message to the community Superintendent Myra Berry acknowledged that school closure decisions are "deeply personal."

"Our schools hold relationships, memories, and a powerful sense of belonging," Berry said. "Throughout the development and discussion of Future Ready, we heard concerns, disagreements, and different convictions about the best path forward. Those perspectives deserve our continued attention, and the voices of our community must help inform the work ahead."

Berry added that the district will now focus on turning the approved changes into "thoughtful, carefully coordinated plans" by working with the affected school communities, preparing for transitions and communicating with the community about the changes.

Earlier this year, the Missouri Department of Elementary and Secondary Education downgraded St. Louis Public Schools to "provisionally accredited." The classification does not affect the operations of schools, Berry said in an undated message.

But it does mean the state "expects continued progress and sustained improvement in areas we have already identified and begun addressing — academic performance, financial stewardship, operational stability, and organizational culture," Berry said.

Kit's most established creators run real companies, with teams, large lists, dedicated sending needs, and a buying process that our self-serve motion wasn't built to serve. Because this part of our business is growing, we're looking for a Senior Product Marketing Manager, Scaling Creators to own how Kit wins, lands, and expands these businesses through a sales-assisted motion that sits alongside our self-serve product.

Our sales leadership, on who we sell to, the deal toolkit, enablement, and win/loss

Account Executives and Solutions Consultants, on demo narratives, discovery, and technical and migration proof

Product, on packaging, launches, and roadmap input

RevOps and analytics, on how accounts get routed to sales, attribution, and pricing decisions

Creator Success, on case studies, customer references, and expansion plays

Demand gen, content, and events, on campaigns and events for established creators

Your work has a direct impact on helping Kit reach $100,000,000 ARR while protecting the self-serve engine that got us here.

Segmentation and buyer definition: turning firmographic and product usage data into a clear picture of who to sell to and when

Sales enablement that reps actually use: demo narratives, discovery guides, business cases, and battlecards

#6922. Time to Break Down That Major Twist at the End of ‘Campus Drivers’ and How It Differs From the Books

The Prime Video series takes a major turn from C.S. Quill’s series.

If—after watching the sixth episode of Campus Drivers—you wondered whether there were still more episodes to come, I’ve got bad news: That really was the finale. Season 1 of the French series from Prime Video ends with a twist that leaves plenty of questions, especially about Lane and Lois’s future. It’s a choice that even surprised those familiar with the book series by C.S. Quill, because the TV ending takes a different direction from the novel’s.

But what exactly happens in the final episode? And, above all, why does Lane decide to leave for Los Angeles right when his relationship with Lois finally seemed destined to take off? Let’s get into it.

In the season 1 finale, we discover that Lane leaves with his mother to move to L.A. and attend film school—a decision that, at first glance, seems to come out of nowhere, especially considering the bond that has formed with Lois over the course of the episodes. Behind this choice, however, there is a secret motivation: Lane’s mother does not approve of the path her son wants to take and believes his career choice is not the right one, but she accepts it on the condition that he leave Bellerose and end his relationship with Lois; otherwise, she is prepared to interfere with Lois’s scholarship, since she considers her an unsuitable partner for Lane.

At that point, Lane is faced with an impossible decision: stay beside the person he is falling in love with, or sacrifice his own happiness to allow her to continue studying medicine at a prestigious university.

Lane chooses the second option. He agrees to leave with his mother, thereby giving up the healthiest relationship of his life just when the two of them were finally beginning to truly grow closer.

The season’s final scene makes the separation even more concrete. In fact, we see movers carrying the furniture out of Lane’s apartment: an image that confirms his departure and the fact that the move to the United States is not just a threat or a possibility, but a decision that has now been made.

What’s more, Lois does not know the real reason for Lane’s departure. She does not know that he agreed to leave only to protect her future and allow her to continue her studies. In her eyes, that separation therefore risks seeming like a personal choice, a renunciation of their story just when it could have begun. At this point, the series departs considerably from the novel: the TV ending changes the protagonists’ journey and chooses to interrupt their story at a particularly delicate point, leaving the most important issue unresolved: the two protagonists did not get their romantic happy ending.

The ending seems designed precisely to leave the door open to a possible sequel (with the two of them still as major protagonists) and to heighten the drama—although for now, an official renewal has yet to be announced. Here’s hoping we will see Lane and Lois again in a second season!

#6917. Here’s How Much the Mirrorball Trophy Is Worth on ‘Dancing With the Stars’

We’re just a few weeks out from the Dancing With the Stars season 35 finale (it goes down on November 24), which means one couple is about to walk away with the Len Goodman Mirrorball Trophy. You know, the giant disco ball that everyone hoists above their heads after winning?

And if you’re wondering how much it’s worth, the answer is PRICELESS, obviously. But also, kind of a lot.

The mirrorball itself is incredibly cheap. Production designer James Yarnell told People in 2017, “No matter how much we put into the trophy, how much we spend, it always has a $10 mirrorball on top.” The show does throw down for the base, however. Winner Kaitlyn Bristowe told Trading Secrets that, all together, the trophy is worth around $10,000.

Apparently Hollywood 3D Printing—who uses “3D printing technologies combined with advanced finishing capabilities such as detailed painting, electroplating, and vacuum plating”—is responsible for making the Mirrorball Trophies, which they confirmed on Instagram in 2023.

Meanwhile, this glitzy statue has come a long way since the early days of Dancing With the Stars. Yarnell said he made the original Mirrorball Trophy (which Kelly Monaco won) from a brass lamp, a wooden base, and some tape. And it stayed that way for four full seasons! Per Yarnell, “They stopped making the lamp at Lamps Plus, so we’d run out of options and had to start casting it and put a heavy acrylic base with a brass stem.”

As for whether or not celebs and pros keep their trophies, it presumably depends on the person. But Derek Hough is so attached to his collection that he literally travels with them on tour and puts them in the lobby for fans to enjoy.

#6913. The ‘Campus Drivers’ Finale May Have Revealed Who Season 2 Will Be About, and It’s a Change From the Books

Lois and Lane’s unfinished story could signal a major shift.

The season 1 finale of Campus Drivers left viewers with several questions, one of them being: Who will the protagonists of the next season be? Prime Video’s French-language series is based on the book series by C.S. Quill, but it has already demonstrated that it doesn’t necessarily intend to follow the novels’ path to the letter. And the very way Lane and Lois’s story ends could foreshadow an important narrative choice for the future of the adaptation.

As a reminder: In the series finale, Lane decides to leave for Los Angeles with his mother in order to allow Lois to keep her scholarship. Their story is cut short just when it seemed destined to turn into something more, leaving several questions open that could become central in a second season.

However, season 1 does also end with a twist involving Donovan. Without giving too much away about what happens in the second book, Crash Course, which is centered on Donovan, the ending sees (spoiler!) Donovan’s dad collapse during a basketball practice. Still, Lois and Lane remain at the center of the narrative with a story still entirely unresolved…and this choice may not be accidental.

The choice to keep Lane and Lois at the center of the story could suggest that Campus Drivers will not necessarily follow the narrative model of Off Campus. In the series based on Elle Kennedy’s novels, each season is designed to mainly tell the story of a new couple, giving space to different protagonists within the same narrative universe.

For Campus Drivers (despite the series having the same structure), the direction could be different: keeping Lane and Lois as central figures in the coming seasons as well, gradually developing the love stories of the other characters and giving them more and more space. Based on the season 1 ending, it’s possible that it will instead use an increasingly ensemble structure, in which the protagonists’ stories intertwine and become richer episode after episode, without necessarily making way for a new main couple each season.

This does not rule out the possibility that, further down the line, other characters could take on an even more important role. As early as the third season, for example, there could be room to explore the secondary love stories in greater depth. For now, however, these are only hypotheses; Prime Video has not yet confirmed what the structure of any future chapters will be.

If the series is renewed, it is reasonable to expect Lane and Lois to maintain a central role. The first season finale left their relationship hanging, and it would be difficult to imagine a follow-up that does not pick up precisely from what happened between them—especially because six episodes felt like too few to fully develop a dynamic between the leads.

The choice not to follow the structure of the novels could also be a way to differentiate Campus Drivers from Off Campus, especially since both series share the same platform and target the same audience of fans.

At the moment, a second season has not yet been officially confirmed. However, considering the attention the series is receiving and the narrative potential left open by the finale, it would not be surprising to see new updates arrive soon.

#6910. Trump administration puts end to mandated boost in Head Start pay

Let K-12 Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

In a new final rule, HHS is rolling back a Biden-era effort to keep salaries competitive and reduce high staff turnover in the early education program.

The Trump administration is ending efforts to boost pay and benefits for Head Start employees, according to a final rule from the U.S. Department of Health and Human Services scheduled to be published Monday. Those efforts would have seen most Head Start teachers earn an additional $10,000 in annual salaries to align with pay for other public preschool educators.

The final rule reverses a 2024 Biden-era rule that supporters said would have helped Head Start early childhood education programs recruit and retain qualified staff and provide consistent quality programming for children and families. The 2024 rule for increased wages and benefits would have been fully implemented in 2031.

Instead, the final rule set to publish on Monday removes those requirements, calling the directive "costly and overly prescriptive for Head Start programs and staff." HHS' Administration for Children and Families, which administers the Head Start program, said its final rule will yield more than $2 billion in cost savings as it restores "greater flexibility to local Head Start programs."

Removing the wage and benefit requirements will prevent the elimination of 106,000 Head Start classroom slots.

Average Head Start teacher salaries range from $26,964 in Mississippi to $79,853 in the District of Columbia, according to a 2024 Urban Institute brief.

The change is just one part of a broader, ongoing effort by the Trump administration to overhaul the Head Start program.

The National Head Start Association, a nonprofit organization that represents Head Start families, providers and educators, said in a Friday statement that underfunding is straining the program's ability to pay staff competitively while maintaining student enrollment levels.

NHSA also said that removing federal wage and benefit requirements "does not change competitive pressures or eliminate the staffing shortages that continue to limit some programs' ability to open classrooms and serve more eligible children and families."

NHSA Executive Director Yasmina Vinci said in a statement, "A strong Head Start program depends on a stable, qualified workforce, and programs must have the resources necessary to recruit, retain, and support the professionals who serve children and families every day."

In a statement responding to the final rule, the National Association for the Education of Young Children said, "HHS frames this as a choice between paying educators and serving children, but a funded slot without a qualified teacher serves no one."

The final rule is to take effect 60 days after publication.

#6907. Just Turn the Heat On? That Might Be Why Your Skin Suddenly Feels So Dry

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

A few weeks ago, I rented a big cabin outside of the city with friends. It was the first cold weekend in New York, the kind where you might actually need to throw on the heavy jackets that were too much for those 55-degree fall days. Nestled in this mountain house, we cozied up around a fire in fleece sweatshirts and fuzzy blankets. Before bed, we turned the heat on. I slept cozy like a baby in the womb, but immediately upon waking, my skin felt tight and dry. I coated myself in moisturizer, but it was no use. My face—which is usually oily and acne-prone—was dull and rough, like it’d never produced a drop of oil in its 29 years.

At first, I thought it was a sign of the times. I’m about to turn 30, and my skin’s just getting drier. But when I headed back to Brooklyn, where my stuffy apartment produces enough natural heat on its own to not need the radiator cranked up, my skin went back to normal.

I’ve heard of this phenomenon before—when your skin gets dehydrated once you turn on the heat in your house—but this was my first experience. And I was determined to get to the bottom of it before my radiator goes on for good and the winter uglies take over. Two dermatologists weighed in—and it looks like this isn’t necessarily preventable but totally treatable.

“Indoor heating lowers the relative humidity in the air, which makes moisture evaporate from your skin more quickly,” says dermatologist Annette Czernik, MD. “Our skin naturally produces oils that help protect it and keep moisture in, but sometimes that's not enough when the air is really dry.” You might apply moisturizer and feel like you didn’t even do anything, she adds.

You’re also not always in the clear if you have oily skin, says Dr. Czernik. “A lot of people don't realize that your skin can be oily and dehydrated at the same time,” she explains. “This is because your skin can still produce oil while losing moisture from the dry air.”

You may also feel tightness, roughness, stinging, flaking, and even itching. “As your skin loses more moisture, its protective barrier can become weaker,” says Dr. Czernik, hence why you can experience some irritation as your skin isn’t as strong enough to fight it off. The air can also make your skin look dull or even feel more sensitive, since it’s working so hard to hold onto the moisture it needs, explains Dr. Czernik. “In more severe cases, you might experience redness, peeling, or even small cracks in your skin,” she adds.

Naturally, with colder air outside, your skin might get drier too, only exacerbating the issue. “When the temperature drops outside, the air usually holds less moisture,” says Dr. Czernik. “Then, when you go indoors, you're exposed to heated air that can also be very dry.” You’re basically losing moisture on both sides, no matter what you do. “Going back and forth between the cold outdoors and warm indoors can make that dryness more noticeable,” she adds. “This is why someone might have perfectly normal skin during the summer but struggle with dryness all winter.”

“When you turn the heat on, the air inside your home can become very dry,” explains dermatologist Ariel Ostad, MD. And one of the easiest ways to add moisture back into the air is a humidifier. “This can help slow down how quickly your skin loses water throughout the day or night,” Dr. Ostad adds.

He recommends getting a bedside humidifier, like the Canopy Bedside Humidifier, for your bedroom and running it while you’re sleeping. “You may notice that your skin feels less tight and dry when you wake up in the morning,” he says. “It can also help with dry lips and irritated skin.” But remember to clean it regularly, as bacteria and mold can build up in your device.

“If your skin starts getting drier, I would first look at the products you're using every day,” says Dr. Ostad. Both Dr. Ostad and Dr. Czernik recommend switching to a thicker moisturizer. Ideally, find one that both moisturizes (meaning it contains ingredients that pull water into your skin, like squalane, hyaluronic acid, panthenol, and glycerin) and seals in hydration (aka ingredients that act as a physical barrier to reduce moisture loss, including ceramides, oils, butters, and silicones). But you could also opt for two moisturizers nightly. I prefer to use a lightweight moisturizer, then follow it up with a barrier cream.

“It's also a good idea to apply your moisturizer right after washing your face or getting out of the shower,” adds Dr. Czernik. If your skin is still damp, even better. This helps pull all that water into your skin (which helps plump it up and decrease dullness), plus it eliminates time for moisture to evaporate from your skin.

Just as hot air can pull water from your skin, so can hot water, explains Dr. Czernik. It doesn’t mean you need to do a daily cold plunge, but opt for mild to warm water temperatures, as scalding water can also increase skin irritation.

Some of the products in your routine could actually be making your dryness worse. Put down all the strong exfoliants, whether it’s your salicylic acid cleanser, an acid-based peel pad, or a retinol serum.

Instead, Dr. Ostad suggests using a gentle, fragrance-free cleanser “that won't strip your skin of its natural oils.” And if you love your exfoliants, you don’t have to ditch them completely, he adds, but you might want to cut back to once or twice per week at most. “You don't need to completely change your skincare routine, but making a few small adjustments can really help during the colder months,” he says.

“If your skin is just a little dry, you can usually manage it at home with a good moisturizer,” says Dr. Ostad. “However, if the dryness continues for a few weeks despite changing your skincare routine, I would recommend seeing a dermatologist.” If you notice your skin becomes extremely itchy or irritated, that’s a good sign to see a doctor too. “Cracking, bleeding, or painful peeling are signs that something more serious could be going on,” adds Dr. Ostad.

Beth Gillette is the beauty editor at Cosmopolitan with eight years of experience researching, writing, and editing skin, hair, makeup, nail, and fragrance stories.

Beth Gillette is the beauty editor at Cosmopolitan, where she covers skincare, makeup, hair, nails, and more across digital and print. She can generally be found in bright eyeshadow furiously typing her latest feature or hemming and hawing about a new product you "have to try." Prior to Cosmopolitan, she wrote and edited beauty content as an Editor at The Everygirl for four years. Follow her on Instagram for makeup selfies and a new hair 'do every few months.

The ideal candidate is a MD or DO who believes in evidence-based hormone replacement therapy (HRT) and is passionate about educating women on perimenopause, menopause, and treatment options.

We’re looking for someone who is comfortable on camera, relatable, and able to communicate medical information in an engaging and approachable way. An established social media presence is preferred but not required. #6895. Everything to Know About Nicholas Hoult’s Wife, Bryana Holly

They just made their red carpet debut at the premiere of Nosferatu.

Nicholas Hoult’s latest flick How to Rob a Bank is about to hit theaters, which means the actor is in the thick of a press tour for the project and making rounds on red carpets.

He made a stop at Jake Shane’s podcast, Therapuss, during his press tour on November 11 and made rare comments about his wife—yes, wife—Bryana Holly.

I know what you’re thinking: How long has it been since Nicholas Hoult and Jennifer Lawrence called it quits, and when did he start dating Bryana? Ahead, find everything we know about Nicholas Hoult’s wife, Bryana Holly.

Bryana is signed to Wilhemina Models and occasionally poses for lingerie, swimsuit, and clothing brands. She also starred in Hoodie Allen’s music video for “Movie” in 2015:

If Bryana looks a little familiar, you may recognize her from her past relationships. The model was romantically linked to Brody Jenner in 2013 before she dated 5 Seconds of Summer drummer Ashton Irwin from 2015 to 2016.

The pair has been together since 2017, and in 2024, Nicholas confirmed they secretly tied the knot.

At the Governors Awards in November 2024, Nicholas called Bryana his “wife” while talking to E! News. “Afterwards, I remember I had my wife’s nails imprinted on my hand,” he said of her reaction to Nosferatu.

A few days later, he confirmed they were married to People, saying, “I feel like it’s fairly clear from my comments.”

Remember that Therapuss interview I mentioned earlier? Well, Nicholas shared the story of how he met his wife in 2016.

“I was shooting a movie,” he said during his convo with Jake. “We were kind of filming and shooting a Halloween party at the same time. And then we had actually a really fun…it was kind of an overcommitment first date.”

He shared that he and Bryana started chatting about their music taste and decided to go to the Desert Trip music festival (which he claimed is “affectionately known” as “Oldchella). They apparently bonded over listening to artists like The Rolling Stones, Paul McCartney, Bob Dylan, and The Who.

“We didn't really know each other, but we both wanted to go,” he explained. “We went to dinner once before just to be like, ‘Let’s make sure neither of us are complete lunatics.’”

Nicholas brought Bryana as his date to the Nosferatu premiere in L.A. back in 2024. And, if you were wondering, this marks their first red carpet appearance after being together for seven years.

Bryana and Nicholas welcomed their first child, Joaquin, in 2018. He’s been seen out and about with the Skins alum and has even made a rare appearance on Bryana’s Instagram (which is now private, womp).

The couple then quietly welcomed their second child in 2022, and according to Nicholas’ How to Rob a Bank co-star, Pete Davidson, they also gave birth to twins at some point.

“He’s a great dad, father of four,” Pete told Entertainment Tonight. “Four under what? 7 [years old] ... which is nuts.”

He also praised Nicholas for how much he catered to his family while filming.

“Elsie was pregnant while we were filming and he just had his twins,” Pete continued. “He would fly back every weekend to go see his family, and that was very inspiring and awesome.”

#6892. What Does the Winner of ‘Dancing With the Stars’ Get, Exactly?

It’s a very different situation for the pro vs. the celeb.

Dancing With the Stars is wrapping up season 35 and someone is about to walk away with a glittery mirrorball trophy and a bunch of money. Actually several someones are going to walk away with a bunch of money, because Dancing With the Stars shares the love between multiple contestants when it comes to winnings. If you have questions, we have answers, so let’s get to it.

Nope. The winner of Dancing With the Stars doesn’t get extra money for winning—they just get the Mirrorball Trophy. However, they do get financially compensated for their time on the show—within an established payment structure.

The cast makes money for each week they remain on the show. According to Variety, everyone takes home $125,000 for the rehearsal period, as well as the first two weeks DWTS airs—meaning, even if you’re voted off you still get paid. The remaining celebs then get bonuses each week they continue.

A while back, there were reports (via Variety) that contestants got an additional $10,000 for weeks three and four, an additional $20,000 for weeks five and six, an additional $30,000 for weeks seven and eight, and then another $50,000 for weeks nine and ten.

This means that all the final celebs who make it to the Dancing With the Stars finale earn $295,000, regardless of whether or not they win.

Pros earn a per-episode salary. We have a full breakdown this week, but according to Us Weekly the highest-paid dancers (aka the pros who have been around for the longest) make around $5,000 an episode.

But there’s no extra baked-in “prize” for the pros if they secure a win, though there is a bonus. Jenna Johnson explained how it works to The Morning After podcast, saying “Whoever makes it to the finale, you all get paid up until then. And you do get a nice bonus. If you win, it’s not like you get a million dollars. You’re just getting a cute trophy together.”

Jenna also confirmed that pros who are voted out earlier are “guaranteed until a certain amount of weeks” pay. Which is something Brandon Armstrong’s wife, Brylee, confirmed on TikTok last year, saying “Brandon is still very much a pro on the show. Just because his partner was eliminated doesn’t mean that he just gets the boot and he’s gotta pack his bags and go all the way back to Utah.” Brylee added that “Dancing with the Stars takes care of their people, so no need to worry.”

#6891. Second Wave Delivery Systems: DRG and Clinical Appeals Specialist

This position is designed as a cross-functional revenue integrity role. While primary responsibilities focus on clinical and DRG appeals, the specialist is expected to support DRG validation audits, coding compliance reviews, clinical validation initiatives, and denial-prevention activities during periods of lower appeals volume. Flexibility and the ability to work across multiple revenue cycle disciplines are essential requirements of the position.

Review denied claims involving DRG downgrades, clinical validation and coding denials.

Conduct a comprehensive analysis of medical records, coded data, physician documentation, laboratory results, imaging findings, treatments, and patient outcomes to identify appeal opportunities.

Draft clear, persuasive, evidence-based appeal letters using clinical indicators, coding guidelines, payer policies, Coding Clinic guidance, and CMS regulations.

Prepare first-level and second-level appeals and reconsideration requests for commercial, Medicare, Medicare Advantage, and Medicaid payers.

Defend the clinical validity of diagnoses commonly targeted by payers, including sepsis, acute respiratory failure, acute kidney injury, malnutrition, encephalopathy, and other MCC/CC conditions, using evidence-based practice and literature.

Validate coding accuracy, DRG assignment, principal diagnosis selection, present-on-admission indicators, and reimbursement determinations.

Research and incorporate peer-reviewed clinical literature, specialty society guidance, and regulatory references to support appeal positions.

Track appeal outcomes, recovered revenue, overturn rates, and denial trends for reporting and continuous improvement.

Participate in client education initiatives focused on denial prevention, documentation improvement, and coding accuracy.

Maintain productivity during fluctuations in denial volume by independently conducting DRG audits, coding compliance reviews, clinical validation assessments, pre-bill revenue integrity reviews, and denial-prevention initiatives to support organizational revenue cycle objectives

Current RN license or professional coding credential required.

Bachelor’s degree in nursing, Health Information Management, or a related healthcare field preferred.

Minimum of five years of experience in Clinical Documentation Integrity (CDI), inpatient coding, denials management, clinical validation, DRG validation, or revenue cycle operations.

Minimum of three years of experience writing successful payer appeals preferred.

Experience reviewing Medicare, Medicare Advantage, Medicaid, RAC, and commercial insurance denials.

Demonstrated experience working with electronic health record (EHR) systems and encoder applications.

Proficiency with 3M, Epic, Cerner, TruCode, Optum, or similar CDI or coding technologies preferred.

One or more of the following certifications preferred: CCDS, CDIP, CCS, RHIA, RHIT, CPC, or CRC.

Advanced knowledge of ICD-10-CM/PCS coding guidelines, MS-DRG and APR-DRG methodologies, and reimbursement systems.

Strong understanding of clinical documentation integrity principles and clinical validation standards.

Exceptional analytical and critical-thinking skills, with the ability to interpret complex clinical records.

Excellent written communication skills, including the ability to develop concise, persuasive, and technically sound appeal letters.

Knowledge of CMS regulations, payer audit methodologies, Coding Clinic guidance, and industry best practices.

Ability to prioritize workload independently and

Demonstrated ability to function effectively in a flexible, cross-trained role, transitioning between denials and appeals activities, DRG validation, coding compliance auditing, clinical validation reviews, and other revenue integrity functions based on business needs

Clinical Expertise: Interprets clinical indicators, treatment patterns, and disease processes to support diagnosis validation.

Appeals Writing: Develops well-supported, evidence-based arguments designed to maximize overturn success rates.

Analytical Thinking: Identifies weaknesses in denial rationales and opportunities for reimbursement recovery.

Collaboration and Flexibilty: Partners effectively with physicians, CDI teams, coding professionals, and revenue cycle stakeholders.

Regulatory Knowledge: Applies CMS regulations, coding guidance, and payer policies accurately and consistently.

Results Orientation: Focuses on maximizing recovered revenue and reducing recurring denial patterns.

#6888. ‘Rivals’ Season 2’s Shocking Tony Baddingham Scene, Explained—And What Happens Next

David Tennant’s ruthless TV boss has officially reached breaking point.

Warning: Major spoilers for Rivals season 2, part 2 ahead.

Just when we thought things couldn't get any more dramatic in Rutshire, Rivals has delivered one of its most shocking moments yet. The second half of season 2 has finally landed on Disney+ and Hulu, picking up in the aftermath of Lady Monica Baddingham's (Claire Rushbrook) devastating death. And while her husband Tony (David Tennant) is struggling to come to terms with losing his wife, his grief is taking a seriously disturbing turn.

Because, in a scene that nobody saw coming, the ruthless Corinium Television boss takes a rifle to Monica's beloved peacocks. Yes, you read that correctly.

It's a particularly brutal moment, even by Tony's standards, and one that reveals just how far he's spiraling following his wife's death.

But what exactly pushed him to breaking point? And what does it mean for the rest of the season?

When we last visited Rutshire, Monica had been killed in a car accident after discovering that Tony was having an affair with Maud O'Hara (Victoria Smurfit).

The latest episodes pick up in the aftermath of her death, with Tony struggling to process his grief while facing the consequences of his infidelity.

While the TV boss has never exactly been known for his kindness, the scene takes his cruelty to another level.

The peacocks are a reminder of Monica and the life they shared, making his decision to kill them feel particularly vicious.

Rather than dealing with the pain of losing his wife, Tony appears to direct his anger toward something she loved.

Speaking to Deadline about the latest episodes, David and executive producer Dominic Treadwell-Collins discussed Tony's increasingly destructive behavior following Monica's death.

He told the outlet: "To lose your life partner is devastating. Even for someone who’s not always the most morally engaged human, I think it’s bewildering and it’s distressing for him. He’s rudderless without her, so I think it’s quite a plausible reaction.

"He’s a man who doesn’t have a particularly well-oiled path to his own emotions, so I think the fact that such a traumatic moment should end with some kind of violent exorcism makes perfect sense. I think that’s who Tony is, unfortunately."

For someone who has spent much of the series manipulating those around him, Tony suddenly finds himself unable to control the one thing that matters most: his own life.

Of course, there's also the small matter of his affair with Maud, which continues to complicate an already messy situation.

If you were hoping Monica's death might inspire Tony to turn over a new leaf, we wouldn't hold our breath.

Despite everything that's happened, Tony remains determined to maintain his grip on Corinium Television, with his bitter rivalry against Declan O'Hara (Aidan Turner) showing no signs of slowing down.

And things are about to get even more complicated now that Declan has discovered the truth about Tony's affair with Maud.

The revelation leads to an explosive confrontation during the Bonfire Night celebrations, leaving Declan and Maud's marriage hanging in the balance.

Meanwhile, Tony's relationship with his son Archie remains fractured, and his increasingly unpredictable behavior raises questions about just how far he's prepared to go to protect his position.

With the battle for the television franchise reaching a critical point, Tony could find himself facing consequences both professionally and personally.

And given what we've just witnessed, we're slightly terrified to see what he does next.

The first three episodes of Rivals season 2, part 2 are available to stream on Disney+ and Hulu, with the remaining episodes arriving weekly.

#6881. Swimply: Regulatory Affairs Manager

Swimply is the marketplace that lets homeowners rent out their private pools, hot tubs, courts and backyards by the hour. Thousands of hosts across the United States and Canada use Swimply to share space they already own with families and small groups in their community.

Because hourly backyard rentals are new, most local codes were written before they existed. Towns, counties, health departments and homeowners associations regularly try to fit Swimply into rules written for hotels, public pools, home businesses or overnight short-term rentals. We are building a dedicated regulatory function to manage those matters at scale and to move the rules in our favor, state by state.

You will own the day-to-day operation of the program and work directly with our General Counsel, our CEO, our Head of Operations and the outside lobbyists and local counsel we retain.

The Regulatory Affairs Manager runs Swimply's regulatory program end to end. Half of the job is operational: every notice a host or the company receives is logged, answered and tracked to a close. The other half is strategic: finding the bills, partners and officials that let us fix the rules for a whole state at once instead of town by town.

No forms. Just smart, optimized applications.

#6879. Meet the Cast and Characters of ‘The Body’

The Body, the latest Netflix series set in the naughty aughties, as they say, has already created some buzz. A spooky, queer coming-of-age story with lots and lots of dancing?! What more could you want in that eerie time between Halloween and the winter holidays? Since several of the cast members are relatively unknown, here’s what you need to know about the cast and characters of The Body.

The series follows a dance team at a Catholic school in the early 00s who are struck by visions, mass hysteria, and possible other supernatural phenomena. We’ve got a sneaking suspicion that it will satisfy your Yellowjackets withdrawals cravings in more ways than one. The Body was created by Quinn Shephard, who previously adapted the series Under The Bridge for Hulu and made the movies Not Okay and Blame, which she also starred in alongside Nadia Alexander and Chris Messina.

To promote the series, Netflix gave each of the main girls saintly taglines. You decide how tongue-in-cheek they are meant to be in context when you watch. Anya’s is “Our Lady of Perpetual Confliction.”

Not to start this off with a tongue-twister, but Kristina Bogic played Gracie Darling in the Australian series Playing Gracie Darling. You can watch the similarly eerie series on Netflix. Her only other credit is a short film called Howl.

Lisbeth’s tagline is “The Snake in the Garden.” Oooh!

Geena Meszaros played a character called “Oatmeal Beth” on the Netflix series Wayward and a character called Bonnie Sue West in the 2024 film The Order. Her other credits include Soul’s Road, In Cold Light, Under the Banner of Heaven, the Freevee original High School, and the limited series A Teacher.

Elise’s tagline is “She Whose Heart Does Not Waver.” Aww?

Sara Boustany’s first acting job on screen was two episodes of Pen15... no big deal!! She also played a teenage version of Sarah Shahi’s character in Dolly Parton’s Heartstrings and “Isabel” in the 2025 movie Hello Beautiful.

Leo is one of the few male characters from the adjoining boy’s school.

Jackson Kelly played David Saunders, the teenage patient/suspect on the first season of The Pitt. He also played Marcus on the limited series Imperfect Women. Jackson has also popped up on shows like The Good Doctor, Station 19, The Righteous Gemstones, and George and Mandy’s First Marriage.

Grace’s tagline is “The Patron Saint of Sacrifice.” Roger that.

Sofia Wylie arguably became the female lead of High School Musical: The Musical: The Series after Olivia Rodrigo left to become, well, Olivia Rodrigo. So you know she’s a triple threat. Prior to HSMTMTS, she competed on So You Think You Can Dance and played Buffy on the Disney Channel series Andi Mack. More recently, Sofia starred in the Netflix fantasy film The School for Good and Evil and played a supporting role in the Prime Video romance film The Map That Leads To You.

Maddie’s tagline is “The Disciple of Sacred Truth.” Well well well!

Shirley Chen is an indie darling in the making you’ve seen in films like Slanted, Didi, Quiz Lady, and Beast Beast. Her television credits also include two episodes of Parenthood, two episodes of Speechless, and one episode of The Resident.

Catherine is another notable adult character as she is Lisbeth and Leo’s mom.

Louisa Krause is a stage and screen actress you know best from her recurring roles on Billions and Ray Donovan and her lead role as “Anna” on the second season of The Girlfriend Experience. Her big screen credits include Superman, Dark Waters, Woman Walks Ahead, and Martha Marcy May Marlene. In the theatre world, she’s best known for playing Rose in several premiere productions of Annie Baker’s play The Flick.

Father Franklin is, naturally, a member of the administration as the high school’s religious leader and is also Grace’s uncle.

Nnamdi Asomugha is a former NFL player who played for the Eagles, the 49ers, and the Raiders. He’s also an Independent Spirit Award nominated actor for Crown Heights. His film and television credits include The Good Nurse, Sylvie’s Love, and a guest star appearance on Friday Night Lights, among others. He’s set to star alongside Jessica Chastain in The Savant. Nnamdi is also an accomplished stage actor who made his off-Broadway debut in Good Grief in 2018 and his Broadway debut two years later in A Soldier’s Play.

Gabby Windey is a legendary member of both Bachelor Nation (and so far the only member of the runner-up to co-lead to married-to-a-woman pipeline) and The Traitors castle. She’s also a former ICU nurse, a Dancing With The Stars runner-up, and hosts a hilarious podcast called Long Winded. This is her acting debut, but next, you’ll see her in Molly Gordon’s upcoming film Peaked alongside Owen Thiele, Emma Mackey, Simone Ashley, and Connor Storrie.

Tyler is a younger, male student in the series.

Marcel Ruiz played Alex Alvarez on the popular reboot of One Day At A Time for al four seasons. He actually won an Imagen Award for best young actor in 2017, NBD. Since then he starred in the movie Breakthrough and will be featured in the upcoming historical drama Porto Rico.

#6874. These Tinted SPFs Protect Your Under-Eyes and Conceal Dark Circles

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Sunscreen is always important, but when one product can also brighten your complexion and take the place of your favorite concealer, it becomes impossible to forget. Tinted SPF offers the best of both worlds, helping protect your skin from the sun while giving it the appearance of a more even complexion. And when it’s made specifically for the eye area, it can make one of the most delicate parts of your skin care routine feel a whole lot easier.

“Tinted sunscreens are one of those rare beauty products where the cosmetic benefit and the dermatological benefit really align,” says Dr. Pearl Ugwu-Dike, a board-certified dermatologist. “You’re protecting your skin from tomorrow’s sun damage while making it look good today.” As for why they’re especially useful under the eyes, the skin in that area is some of the most delicate that we have on our body. “That makes it particularly susceptible to visible signs of photoaging, like fine lines, crepiness, hyperpigmentation,” Dr. Ugwu-Dike says. “It’s also often a place that gets neglected when applying sunscreen, conventionally.” That’s why the best tinted SPFs for under-eyes are so special: They offer protection while providing immediate color correction and a brighter-looking appearance, helping to camouflage under-eye bags and dark circles.

Of course, the right formula depends on how much coverage you want, your skin type, and how your under-eye area tends to behave throughout the day. Some options are sheer and brightening, while others can pull double duty as a true concealer. Ahead, we’ve rounded up the best tinted SPFs for under-eyes so you can find the one that fits seamlessly into your morning routine.

This is what happens when an eye cream, sunscreen, and concealer all decide to overachieve. “I love that it addresses two of my biggest concerns with the under-eye area, which are pigmentation and photoaging,” says Dr. Pearl Ugwu-Dike, an NYC-based board-certified dermatologist. “I also really like that it combines peptides, hyaluronic acid, and a cooling applicator.”

Key ingredients: Titanium dioxide, zinc oxide, hyaluronic acid, peptides

Customer review: “This is a wonderful product! Skin care, SPF, and even color corrector in one. It goes on smoothly, does not crease, does not sting the eyes. The peachy color gives a natural concealing effect. I can't recommend this enough.”

This product is for anyone who wants their under-eye area to look like they just got eight hours of sleep followed by a facial. “The formulation is really smart,” says Dr. Ugwu-Dike. It contains both zinc oxide and titanium dioxide, along with snow mushroom, peptides, ceramides, and sodium PCA. “The snow mushroom is going to act like a humectant, which is particularly helpful in the delicate skin on the eye area where dehydration can exaggerate fine lines,” she adds.

Key ingredients: Titanium dioxide, zinc oxide, snow mushroom, peptides, ceramides, sodium PCA

Customer review: “This eye corrector feels great going on and covers dark circles very well. Love it and plan to keep purchasing it in the future.”

Some days you get eight hours of sleep, and other days you need a very convincing color corrector. This formula from RMS “relies on sheer, peachy undertones to neutralize the appearance of darkness while allowing skin to look like skin,” says Dr. Ugwu-Dike. “I also really like that it includes caffeine and coffee seed extract in the formula that might temporarily improve the appearance of puffiness.”

Key ingredients: Zinc oxide, caffeine, coffee seed extract

Customer review: “Love this eye cream. It makes the entire eye area look better. I use it every day, and it covers beautifully—no need for concealer over it. It lasts all day. My eyes are very sensitive and this does not bother them at all.”

“I think this formula is perfect for someone who doesn't necessarily have substantial pigmentation under the eyes, but wants to improve a tired or dull appearance,” says Dr. Ugwu-Dike. The lightweight formula approaches under-eye darkness through illumination rather than heavy coverage, delivering a bright appearance that’s never cakey. It also has caffeine for de-puffing and green tea extract for soothing.

Key ingredients: Titanium dioxide, zinc oxide, snow mushroom, peptides, ceramides, sodium PCA

Customer review: “I’ve been using this for nearly a year now and it is my holy grail product! It glides on well for me - no pilling. My skin leans towards dry and sensitive. I’ve never had any issues with this eye cream. I use it in the morning and it brightens beautifully. It acts great as a primer. On no-makeup days, it brightens me up!”

While most products on this list are SPF eye creams with a tint, Ciele flipped the narrative. This formula puts concealer first, infusing the highly pigmented makeup product with SPF 50+. Place it under the eyes or on your blemishes to keep them both hidden and protected.

Key ingredients: Tranexamic acid, zinc oxide, titanium dioxide

Customer review: “My absolute favorite concealer. There’s nothing else out there like it—only formula I feel comfortable putting on blemishes knowing it will conceal (won’t budge!), treat, and protect with SPF. It’s better than any spot treatment I could have found.”

#6873. From Chocolate Chrome to Plaid Prints, These Are the Chicest Thanksgiving Nail Ideas to Try This Year

Your fingertips also deserve to be dressed up for the feast.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

We are officially in the thick of November, which means festive nail art is now on the horizon. And with Thanksgiving just a few weeks away, there's no better time to lean into a playful fall manicure than at your next nail appointment.

I rarely need an excuse to get a fresh set. Still, Thanksgiving feels like a particularly good one to experiment with autumnal designs and shades that are perfectly on-theme for the big feast. Between family dinners and Friendsgiving celebrations, there are plenty of opportunities to show off your nails. “The holiday season is the perfect excuse to embrace maximalist nail art,” says celebrity nail artist Max Estrada. “If your nails want to dress up for dinner, let them.”

Ahead, I chatted with nail artists about their favorite Thanksgiving nail ideas, along with polish recommendations to help you recreate a turkey-day-ready look.

“Thanksgiving is all about family gatherings, cozy evenings, and the warmth of the fall season,” says celebrity nail artist Vikki Swan. “I gravitate toward earthy tones and designs that reflect this atmosphere, so I especially love plaid patterns.” You can choose classic stripes or argyle prints, and go for an all over pattern or a subtle French tip. It’s quite literally the manicure equivalent of your favorite knit sweater.

Let the changing colors of fall foliage be the inspo behind your next manicure. “I’m loving gold leaf and metallic foils for Thanksgiving,” says Estrada. “The irregular placement of the leafing is part of the beauty and you don’t need every nail to look identical.” You’ll be sure to have the most on-theme manicure at the holiday table.

“I love a classic French manicure with a seasonal twist, such as chocolate brown, caramel, or burgundy tips,” says Swan. Start off with a milky or nude base, and simply choose your favorite fall hue for the tip of the nail.

Animal prints in the brown hue family, like tiger, cheetah, and fawn, are an ideal pattern to lean into for November. They won’t feel stale days after your Thanksgiving feast is over, either. There are plenty of print variations to play with, but I especially love the look of a milky base with spots or stripes layered on top in the shape of a French tip.

Both Estrada and Swan love a warm brown polish this time of year, “paired with a subtle pearl chrome finish provides beautiful depth and a soft shimmer to the nails,” Swan says. “Cat-eye effects are another big part of the conversation,” Estrada adds. “They give those rich colors movement and dimension, so the manicure becomes interesting through the finish itself.” You can’t go wrong with OPI’s Cliffside Karaoke or Zoya’s Thatcher.

Why settle on one fall hue when you can wear a different shade on every finger? “Using different shades from the same color family across the nails and transitioning from lighter to deeper tones, is a simple yet elegant way to capture the fall mood without intricate nail art,” says Swan. Just choose a few of your favorite polishes, and they don’t need to match perfectly, either.

Jasmine Hyman is the assistant beauty editor at Cosmo who covers all things nails—from the latest design trends to new polish launches. For this story, she consulted celebrity nail artists for their favorite Thanksgiving nail ideas.

#6865. Friday the 13th Just Got a Little Luckier This Year

And you can thank Mercury Retrograde and Venus Retrograde finally coming to an end for that!

Friday the 13th may be tomorrow, but that doesn’t mean that luck isn’t in the air! This year on the longtime “unlucky” day, both Mercury Retrograde and Venus Retrograde (finally!) come to an end within a few hours of each other, bringing relief and clarity to your close relationships and ability to express yourself.

One retrograde is painful and frustrating enough, but over the past few weeks, you’ve navigated two at the same time, which can feel immobilizing at best and chaotic at worst. Fortunately, you’re reaching the finish line! Beyond the retrograde relief, Friday the 13th is actually considered an incredibly magical day, brimming with intuitive breakthroughs, despite its reputation.

For millennia, the number 13 was actually revered by many cultures and traditions all across the globe because of its connection to the average number of lunations we experience every year and also the average number of menstrual cycles a person experiences in a year. It was celebrated as a day to connect with and honor the Goddess. People who have periods are said to experience heightened intuition during their cycle and a strong connection to the other Realms. It’s through these associations that the number 13 carries deep and profound ties to magic, creation, manifestation, and birthing both new life and new desires and visions for your future.

Over centuries, the original meaning was distorted by patriarchal leaders and religions out of fear of collective feminine energy. By concocting false narratives and creating fearful myths about Friday the 13th, the patriarchal forces were able to exercise more control all while divorcing and dispelling many feminine people from reclaiming their own power and agency. Which is actually why many people consider this an “unlucky” day—but it’s really not at all! Once again, the patriarchy was out here ruining everything.

#6863. New Industry Alliance to Work Toward Common Security Model for AI Agents

Okta has announced a coalition bringing together major cloud, security, data and application vendors to create a shared architecture for securing AI agents across enterprise environments.

The 12 founding members of the new Blueprint Alliance are AWS, CrowdStrike, Databricks, Docker, Google Cloud, Lovable, Okta, Proofpoint, Salesforce, ServiceNow, Wiz and Zscaler. GE Appliances and World Central Kitchen are participating as strategic advisors.

The effort expands an earlier Okta framework into an open, multi-vendor reference architecture intended to address a basic problem with enterprise AI agents: An agent can be created in one system, authenticated through another, access tools and data from several others, and be monitored by yet another security platform.

The Alliance is focused less on how agents are built and more on how organizations can govern them consistently across vendor boundaries.

The Blueprint Alliance architecture organizes agent security around four questions: Where are my agents? What can they do? What are they doing? How do I respond?

Those questions translate into several practical areas:

The reference architecture also covers development-related systems. Its whitepaper includes agent frameworks, code repositories, CI/CD environments, code assistants, tool registries, Model Context Protocol servers, and command-line tools among the components that may need to be discovered or governed.

That makes the initiative relevant to development teams deploying agents that interact with internal APIs, MCP tools, databases, SaaS applications, or other agents. The architecture calls for those connections and permissions to remain visible and controllable as agents move from development into production.

A major part of the Alliance's work will be testing how security information and controls can move between products from different vendors.

The initial architecture references several existing standards and protocols, including MCP for agent-to-tool interactions, the Open Cybersecurity Schema Framework for security telemetry, and the Shared Signals Framework and Continuous Access Evaluation Profile for exchanging risk information.

The goal is for one part of an enterprise stack to be able to detect a risk condition and pass that information to another control point that can act on it. Depending on the implementation, that could include revoking access or containing an agent whose behavior has changed.

The Alliance says members will build and test reference integrations and publish results as interoperability work progresses. Specific cross-vendor implementations and performance results were not documented in the launch announcement.

Founding member Lovable also published a developer-oriented explanation of its participation, describing organizations that may have internally built agents, agents imported through SaaS platforms and locally running agents that all need to be inventoried and governed under a common model.

The Alliance's whitepaper recommends that organizations begin with foundational capabilities such as unified logging, telemetry, and agent discovery rather than attempting to deploy every control at once.

The architecture is available for community use, and the Alliance says it will continue evolving as members test integrations and additional participants become involved. Timelines for individual vendor integrations have not been published.

For more information, check out the Okta announcement here.

David Ramel is an editor and writer at Converge 360.

In its latest threat intelligence report, Ontinue found that attackers are increasingly using trusted workplace tools to trick users into giving them access to enterprise networks.

Cybercriminals are moving away from mass phishing scams and focusing on more targeted attacks, often using trusted websites and services to trick victims, according to a new report from KnowBe4.

Anthropic announced it is introducing machine-readable watermarks to text generated by supported Claude models as it moves to comply with European Union AI transparency rules.

OpenAI has introduced Dots, GPT-6 Astra-powered agents that run on their own cloud computers, using connected applications to carry on with assignments between conversations and incorporating users' feedback as they go.

#6859. Meet the Cast and Characters of ‘The Good Daughter’

Excited to watch The Good Daughter on Peacock, but didn’t read the book by Karin Slaughter that it’s based on? Here’s everything you need to know about the cast and characters of The Good Daughter, without giving anything away. Well, maybe one thing. There’s a character who only shows up in one timeline for a reason. You might guess why, but I’m going to spoil it later just so we’re all on the same page.

First of all, no... this series is not connected to The Good Wife, The Good Fight, The Good Doctor, or The Good Place. As mentioned above, The Good Daughter is an adaptation of a bestselling novel that was published in 2017. The story is about two adult sisters who are reunited after a violent attack in their Georgia hometown brings up memories of the murder that tore them apart.

Slaughter herself adapted her book for the screen, penning the teleplays for all six episodes. Steph Green stepped in to direct and executive produce the series. As for the cast, here’s who you’ll see on The Good Daughter and who they play.

Sam, quintessential eldest daughter, left town and became a patent lawyer.

Rose Byrne’s breakout role in Hollywood was as a titular bridesmaid in Bridesmaids, though she had already appeared in films like Sunshine, Troy and Marie Antoinette at that time. That same year, 2011, she played Moira MacTaggert in X-Men: First Class and would reprise that role in a later film. She’s still best known for comedies, however, like Spy, Neighbors, and Neighbors 2 as well as horror movies like 28 Weeks Later and the Insidious films. Television-wise, Byrne played Gloria Steinem in Mrs. America and starred in both Physical and Platonic. She was nominated for an Academy Award for If I Had Legs I’d Kick You and a Tony Award for Fallen Angels.

Meanwhile, younger sister Charlie stayed in town and became a defense attorney.

Meghann Fahy was one of the breakout stars of The White Lotus’ second season, though she also hadn’t come out of nowhere. Prior to the HBO seires, Meghann made her Broadway debut in the Pulitzer Prize winning musical Next to Normal as an understudy and replacement for the character Natalie, then played Sutton on The Bold Type, and also acted in 83 episodes of One Life To Live. Since the HBO series, she has starred in the movie Drop and the Netflix series Sirens as well as The Perfect Couple and Your Monster. She is also a two time Emmy nominee, for both Sirens and The White Lotus.

Audrey Grace Marshall and Drew Cheek play Young Sam and Charlie

Flashbacks show who the sisters were before the trauma that shaped them.

Before The Good Daughter, Audrey Grace Marshall played Young Cassie on The Flight Attendent and was supposed to be in the Buffy the Vampire Slayer reboot. She did play Viv in the Fairly Oddparents live action reboot thing in 2022. Drew Cheek, on the other hand, has only one previous credit: a movie called F*ck Valentine’s Day.

Rusty is the sisters’ father and a former defense attorney with a reputation.

Brendan Gleeson is an Academy Award-nominated and Emmy Award-winning actor. He won the latter for playing Winston Churchill in the miniseries Into the Storm. You’ve seen him in the Harry Potter films, Braveheart, 28 Days Later, Gangs of New York, Troy, In Bruges, Edge of Tomorrow, Paddington 2, and The Banshees of Inisherin. Brendan has not done much television, but did lead Mr. Mercedes from 2017 to 2019, appear in the 2022 series State of the Union, and play Silvermane in Spider-Noir.

Lenore is Rusty’s secretary and the girls’ surrogate mother figure.

This is Harper Steele’s first ever professional acting credit. She is an Emmy winning comedy writer who worked at Saturday Night Live for 13 years and was a head writer for four of those years. Her other screenwriting credits include Eurovision Song Contest: The Story of Fire Saga and The Spoils of Babylon. Harper recently appeared with Will Ferrell in the road trip buddy documentary Will & Harper about their friendship before and after she came out as transgender.

Kelly is a suspect in a school shooting who Charlie chooses to represent.

Shayna Bernardo’s previous credits before The Good Daughter include the Lifetime movie Don’t Sweat The Small Stuff, an episode of Doom Patrol, and the horror movie Sorority Secrets.

Judith is an English teacher at a local middle school.

Paula Malcomson is probably best known for playing Katniss’ understandably checked-out mother Asterid Everdeen in The Hunger Games movies. She was also a series regular on the Battlestar Galactica prequel series Caprica, as well as Deadwood and Ray Donovan. She also recurred on Sons of Anarchy.

Huck is a teacher at the local middle school with a grudge against Rusty.

Jake McDorman is best known for leading roles in shows like Quintuplets, Greek, Manhattan Love Story, Limitless, Murphy Brown, The Right Stuff, Mrs. Davis and Dopesick. He also had a recurring role on Shameless. Movie-wise, you’ve seen him in Happiest Season, Lady Bird, American Sniper, Live Free Or Die Hard, Bring It On: All Or Nothing, and, of course, Aquamarine.

Olivia Williams plays Harriet ‘Gamma’ Quinn

First of all, no, this Harriet was the sisters’ mother and a genius physicist. Spoiler alert: she was violently murdered when Sam and Charlie were young girls.

You may know Olivia Williams from films like Rushmore, the 2003 Peter Pan, Anna Karenina, and An Education. Fun fact: Williams played Moira MacTaggert in X-Men: The Last Stand. She played Camilla Parker-Bowles on two seasons of The Crown and her other television credits include Dollhouse, Manhattan, and Dune: Prophecy. She’s also a prolific stage actress and starred as Nurse Ratched in One Flew Over The Cuckoo’s Nest at London’s Old Vic Theatre this year.

Ben is Charlie’s estranged husband, and also a lawyer. So many lawyers!

Michael Dorman is an Australian actor with many starring roles on television to his name, including shows like Wonderland, For All Mankind, Patriot, Territory, and Joe Pickett. His film credits include Leigh Whannell’s The Invisible Man and Pirates of the Caribbean: Dead Men Tell No Tales.

#6857. AI Platform Behind 12,000 E-mail Breaches Dismantled

The platform moved quickly. Since launching in February, EvilTokens had been tied to more than 12,000 compromised inboxes across over 10,000 organizations worldwide, according to Microsoft. Its victims included businesses and institutions in financial services, healthcare, construction, real estate, wholesale distribution, and higher education.

Microsoft and its partners seized 50 websites used to operate EvilTokens and disabled more than 150 additional domains supporting the service. The Metropolitan Police Service in the United Kingdom also arrested two men on Sept. 11 in connection with the alleged operation.

At the center of EvilTokens was an AI chatbot designed specifically for cybercrime. Once attackers gained access to an inbox, the chatbot could analyze messages, map relationships, identify employees involved in payments, and recommend ways to impersonate trusted contacts.

Microsoft said the platform helped criminals determine "who to target, who to impersonate," and how to get the most money from a compromised organization.

Business e-mail compromise has traditionally required attackers to manually comb through messages, learn how an organization handles payments, and find the right person to impersonate. However, EvilTokens simplified the entire process by automating much of that work.

The tools could quickly sort through a stolen inbox, summarize or translate messages, find invoices and wire-transfer discussions, and identify the people who moved money. EvilTokens could then draft messages that sounded like they came from the victim. It packaged all of this with mailbox access, phishing tools, management dashboards, and even customer support.

The service was sold through Telegram for a $1,500 startup fee, followed by a $500 monthly subscription. Microsoft's complaint also described a bulk e-mail tool that cost $600 a month and a more advanced e-mail delivery platform priced at $1,000 a month.

To break into a target, EvilTokens relied on device-code phishing. Victims were sent to a real Microsoft sign-in page and persuaded to enter an authentication code. The login process looked legitimate because it was, but the code quietly authorized a session controlled by the attacker.

That also made cleanup more complicated. The attackers stole OAuth tokens and authenticated sessions instead of passwords, so a password reset alone might not remove their access. Administrators also needed to revoke the stolen tokens and active sessions.

According to Microsoft's complaint,  EvilTokens used fake Microsoft 365 security notices and other branded templates to direct users into the device authentication flow. Once inside, attackers could use Microsoft Graph and other APIs to search and monitor a mailbox.

The company also found that AI played a role in creating EvilTokens itself. Investigators said much of the platform appeared to have been "vibe coded," with its developers using AI to quickly build and expand the service. EvilTokens reportedly drew on multiple AI models, including improperly integrated services from Groq and OpenAI.

The court authorized Microsoft to take control of or disable domains connected to the operation. Microsoft worked with Cloudflare, Coinbase, OpenAI, Railway, SpyCloud, the Shadowserver Foundation, and TRM Labs on the disruption. Health-ISAC joined the case because healthcare organizations were among the targets.

This marks the Microsoft Digital Crimes Unit's 40th court-authorized disruption and its first targeting an end-to-end, AI-enabled cybercrime service.

EvilTokens' infrastructure may be offline, but the model behind it is unlikely to disappear. The operation showed how AI can turn a stolen inbox into an immediately useful fraud playbook, allowing less-experienced criminals to launch attacks that once required considerable time and skill.

For more information, go to the Microsoft blog post.

Chris Paoli (@ChrisPaoli5) is the associate editor for Converge360.

In its latest threat intelligence report, Ontinue found that attackers are increasingly using trusted workplace tools to trick users into giving them access to enterprise networks.

Cybercriminals are moving away from mass phishing scams and focusing on more targeted attacks, often using trusted websites and services to trick victims, according to a new report from KnowBe4.

Anthropic announced it is introducing machine-readable watermarks to text generated by supported Claude models as it moves to comply with European Union AI transparency rules.

OpenAI has introduced Dots, GPT-6 Astra-powered agents that run on their own cloud computers, using connected applications to carry on with assignments between conversations and incorporating users' feedback as they go.

#6855. These Cashmere Perfumes Are the Equivalent of Quiet Luxury in a Bottle

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Like wrapping yourself in a $400 cashmere sweater.

Not much compares to the snuggly feeling of a cashmere sweater against your skin, especially in cold weather. But if you don’t want to drop the $$$ on a whole cashmere wardrobe (if you do, make sure it’s Quince’s affordable cashmere), the next-best thing is a cashmere perfume.

Obviously, cashmere itself doesn’t have a smell. It’s a fabric, after all! But perfumers have found a way to capture that fluffy, cozy feeling through an accord called “cashmeran”—a blend of fragrance notes designed to evoke the soft, warm sensation of cashmere. Usually, that means it’s aldehydic (like fresh linens and cotton), warm, and a little bit powdery. But perfumes can still fall into the “cashmere” bucket even without a specific accord, usually using notes like creamy sandalwood and vanilla, warm amber and tonka bean, and even cloud-like musk and jasmine.

Smelling like cashmere has been my preferred way to embrace the homey feeling heading into winter. Nestling beside a fire, drinking a hot beverage, and wearing—you guessed it—a cashmere sweater has never been easier, so long as I have one of the below scents sprayed on. Keep reading for Cosmo editors’ top seven picks from brands like Byredo, Orebella, Phlur, and NOYZ.

"I always thought powdery perfumes would make me smell like a grandma, but either I'm inching toward grandma status or Angel Dust by Fugazzi is built different. It's powdery but not from heavy florals. Instead, the scent combines a mix of skin-like cashmere, musk, and amber. It's soft and reminiscent of the inside of a knitwear shop.”—BETH GILLETTE, BEAUTY EDITOR

“It’s hard to find a fragrance that straddles freshness and coziness, but this one does exactly that. Its moniker comes from a Brazilian Portuguese expression that describes the sensation of good vibes, happiness, and optimism, and it’s fair to say this perfume captures that sentiment perfectly. It oozes Brazilian warmth and sunniness and makes me want to book a trip to Rio.”—CELIA SHATZMAN, CONTRIBUTING WRITER

“I rely on body mists immediately after I get out of the shower to lightly fragrance my skin without feeling like too much. This one is my go-to right now (and the inspiration behind my current cashmere fragrance obsession). Even with nothing on (spicy!), my skin smells so toasty. It makes me want to immediately nuzzle into a plush robe.”—BG

“Unmute has a little bit of everything—juiciness from the plum note, spice from the cinnamon, musk from the amber, warmth from the sage, and sweetness from the pistachio—all wrapped in a cozy, comforting vanilla base. There’s something sexy and self-assured about the scent, without feeling too put-on or artificial.”—DANIELLE HARDMAN, CONTRIBUTING WRITER

“Milky perfumes are my favorite category of fragrance lately, and this does the lactonic, creamy fragrance so well. It’s not too sweet or cloying; instead, it leans on resinous amber, oak, and tonka bean to balance the scent. It’s like sitting by a fire, wrapped in the softest knits.”—BG

"I usually associate floral perfumes with spring and summer, but this one is undeniably perfect for cooler weather. Odéon is a smooth, rich rose scent, with a hefty dose of amber and cashmeran that gives it depth. It's inspired by Parisian cafe culture—and since I studied abroad there (hold the eyerolls), I feel qualified to say that this scent embodies the city’s romantic allure. It smells like window shopping in boutiques I can't afford and moonlit strolls along the Seine."—LAUREN GRUBER, COMMERCE EDITOR

“It’s an icon for a reason! Cashmere Mist is the OG of cashmere scents—it’s musky, powdery, and creamy, with soft woods and airy florals. My aunt was obsessed with this perfume in my youth, and I remember sneaking sprays of it whenever I wanted to feel like a ~sophisticated lady~”—BG
